3.14 Medical attention received at the time of birth and death

3.14.1 The complete information for 2019 in respect of birth by type of medical attention received at
the time of delivery has been reported from only 32 States/UTs. As partial information has been
submitted by four States namely, Bihar, Jharkhand, Maharashtra and Sikkim, their data has not been
taken into account while consolidating the data. The distribution of medical attention received at the
time of birth for these States/UTs has been illustrated in Chart 13. While the data shows that majority
of births have been occurred in an institution, either in Government or private hospitals. Institutional
births occurred in about 81.2% of cases, 8.4% of the cases were attended by the
Physicians/Nurse/Midwife. However, about 4.5% of the events are still attended by untrained Mid-
wife/Dai and another 3.2% by others.

                                                                          38
3.14.2 Complete information on Medical Attention received by deceased at the time of death has been
received from 31 States/UTs. Four States namely Bihar, Jharkhand, Maharashtra and Sikkim have
submitted only partial data and therefore their data has not been used to while consolidating the data.
Also one State Haryana has not submitted the requisite information. Chart 14 represents the
distribution of medical attention received by the deceased at the time of death. It is evident from Chart
that 32.1% of the total registered deaths have occurred in institutions and is on a higher side than that
of other places from where the deceased had received medical attention. About 14.4% of Registered
Deaths were reported under Medical Attention Other than Institutions. About 3.0% of the total
registered deaths during 2019 have received medical attention from qualified allopathic professional
and practitioner of other system and 34.5% of deceased did not receive any medical attention at the
time of death.

3.15 Infant Deaths

3.15.1 The infant deaths registered by place of occurrence (Rural /Urban) of the event for the year
2019 has been given in Statement 17. It may be observed that the share of rural area is only 24.5%
while that of urban area is 75.5% in total registered infant deaths during the year. Non-registration of
infant deaths in rural area is a cause of concern which may be due to non-reporting of infant deaths to
the Registrars especially in case of domiciliary events. This figure is exclusive of returns of Jharkhand
due to non-receipt of information from the State.

3.16 Still Births

3.16.1 The World Health Organisation (WHO) defines a still birth as a baby born with no signs of life
at or after 28 weeks' gestation. Still birth, also known as foetal death, has been defined as `death prior
to the complete expulsion or extraction from its mother of a product of human conception, irrespective of
the duration of pregnancy and which is not an induced termination of pregnancy. The death is indicated
by the fact that after such expulsion or extraction, the fetus does not breathe or show any other evidence
of life, such as beating of the heart, pulsation of the umbilical cord, or definite movement of voluntary
muscles. Heartbeats are to be distinguished from transient cardiac contractions; respirations are to be
distinguished from fleeting respiratory efforts or gasps.'

3.16.2 Statement 19 presents still births registered by place of occurrence (Rural /Urban) of the
event for the year 2019 and it may be observed that in total registered still births, share of rural
registered still births is only 41.6% which may be due to poor netting of still births in rural areas. In

urban areas, this share is about 58.4% which may be due to better netting of still births from medical

institutions. This is based on registered events from 35 States/UTs. The data from one State namely
Manipur is not available. State/UT-wise registered still births has been depicted in Table 4.

                    Statement 19: Still Births registered during 2019

                                              40
3.17 Analysis and Findings

Level of Registration (LOR)

3.17.1      The level of registration, defined as the percentage of registered births/deaths to the

births/deaths estimated through SRS, determines the performance of a State / Union Territory in

registration of births and deaths. In the absence of reliable estimates of number of births and

deaths on a year to year basis from any other source, the number of events as revealed from SRS is

used to calculate the level of registration. The level of registration so calculated is very high for

some of the smaller States/Union Territories either due to the lower estimates of vital events than

the actual and due to the fact that SRS estimates of vital events are based on the place of usual

residence while events are registered at the place of occurrence irrespective of the place of

residence.

3.17.2      New population Projections for India and States 2011­2036 based on findings of the

Technical Group on Population Projections constituted by National Commission on Population

Expert were released recently and need was felt to revise the Level of Registration of Births and

Deaths from 2011 onwards as Mid-year population is one of the vital constituents in calculation of

Level of Registration. New Population Projections are more reliable and accurate as the earlier

estimates were based on population census 2001. In the absence of any alternate reliable and

single source to provide yearly intercensal yearly estimates for population of States/UTs and India

previous versions of this report continued with the best available alternative. Now, as the new

population projection report is released the figures for Level of Registration of Births & Deaths for

the years 2011-2019 for States/Union territories and India has been duly revised and included in

the report which are more accurate. One more constituent of Level of Registration is Sample

Registration Survey's Rates (SRS) i.e Birth and Death Rates. As the survey for the year 2019 which

was scheduled for 2020 could not be completed as was affected by spread of COVID-19 pandemic.

For the calculation of Level of Registration for the year 2019, SRS rates for the year 2018 have been

used. Due to revision of population, the data presented in this report for Level of Registration and

Estimated Births and Deaths for the years 2011-2018 are not comparable with earlier reports.

3.17.3      Under this section Level of Registration of Births & Deaths are based on revised mid-

year population as per the recently released projections based on census 2011 unless specified.

The levels of registration of births and deaths for India and States/UTs for the period 2011 to 2019

are shown in Statements 20 and 21 respectively. The level of registration of births has increased to

92.7 in 2019 from 82.4% in 2011 i.e. in last 9 years an increase of about 10.3%. Similarly, the level

of registration of deaths has also increased to 92.0% in 2019 from 66.4% in 2011, showing an

increase of 25.6% over the last 9 years. Chart 15 depicts the trend in level of registration of births

and deaths from 2011 to 2019. Due to continuous efforts of the States and UTs the gap between

Levels of Birth and Death Registration is narrowing down with a difference of 0.7% observed in

2019.

3.17. 9 Kerala, West Bengal, Rajasthan, Haryana, Maharashtra, Karnataka and Andhra Pradesh the

only 7 major States recording more than 90 percent of registered births as well as deaths both in 2018

and 2019. Two States namely Jharkhand and Bihar which were placed in category 50-80% for Level of

Birth Registration, moved up by 1 category. Bihar the only State which was placed in the 25 - 50 %

category in terms of death registration in 2018, moved up 1 category in 2019. No State is under the

category of less than 25 percent of registration of births & deaths during 2018 as well as 2019. 1 State

namely Punjab has come to lower level of 80-90 % during 2019 in terms of Level of Birth Registration.

While in terms of level of Death registration, 2 States namely Telangana and Uttarakhand improved

from 50-80 % in 2018 to highest category i.e above 90% in 2019. During 2019, Madhya Pradesh and

Chhattisgarh improved their position by 1 category up and moved to 80-90 % from 50-80% in 2018 in

terms of level of registration of Deaths.

States/UTs has together been mapped in the Chart 18. The rectangle on the top right corner is the

expected target towards achieving the cent percent registration and status of each States is depicted in

the Chart 18.

3.19 Time Gap in Registration of Birth & Death

3.19.1 On the basis of data made available by States/UTs, the national data table on time gap of birth
and death registrations i.e. within time limit (<21 days), 21-30 days, 30 days to less than 1 year and
After one year has been prepared and may be seen in Statement 24 and 25. The 5 States namely Bihar,
Jharkhand, Maharashtra, Sikkim and Delhi have provided only partial data on Time Gap Birth
Registration and therefore included. On the other hand, 5 States namely Bihar, Jharkhand, Maharashtra,
Sikkim and Delhi have provided only partial data while 1 State Haryana has not submitted the table on
Time Gap Death Registration.
